## 4. THE RIDER R -- committed in 3e33201d (#546); its owed nine-seat review

**The defect (measured).**  `tools/pipeline/check_commit_attribution.py` (precommit section L) reads the last 60
commit subjects, finds ledger row ids in them, and grades each match: a CLOSED row must name the commit in its
`commits` field (whitespace-separated shas, validated elsewhere as 7-40 lowercase hex, or a "no commit" /
"outside the repo" / "ops" note); a subject saying "closed" against a non-closed row is HARD unless the row's
free-text `reopened` field names the commit.  It compared git's `%h` by exact membership (`h in commits`) and by
substring (`h in reopened`).  The rows carry the 7-character form git printed when each closed; after `4264ee20`
git's automatic abbreviation became 8 characters (`git log --abbrev=7` still prints 7 for all 794 commits -- no
collision; git's own minimum grew), so six correctly attributed rows hard-failed and the gate was red for every
commit.  The mirror case failed too: a row written at 8 characters did not match a 7-character `%h`.

**The fix as committed** (`git show --format= 3e33201d -- tools/pipeline/check_commit_attribution.py`, 23+/6-,
INLINED):

+SHA = re.compile(r"(?<![0-9A-Za-z])[0-9a-f]{7,40}(?![0-9A-Za-z])")

+def names(field, full):
+    """True when some 7-40 character hex token in `field` is a prefix of the full hash."""
+    return any(full.startswith(t) for t in SHA.findall(field or ""))

**Two seats reviewed R before commit -- codex (GPT-6 Astra, max) and grok 4.7 (xhigh), both SHIP WITH NOTES,
agreeing on every note.  Their notes, and my dispositions:**

1. Prefix matching presumes the prefix is unique -- true of every current token.  RECORDED.
2. The extractor is shared with the FREE-TEXT `reopened` field, where constructed inputs defeat it: a 7+ letter
   hex-only English word ("effaced") that prefixes a scanned commit's hash reads as a name (the old substring test had
   the same exposure at 7 characters); a sha glued to letters, or followed by an uppercase hex digit, becomes
   invisible (the old test saw it); `_` and non-ASCII letters count as boundaries.  None occurs in the ledger -- two
   rows carry `reopened`, both opening with their sha.  RECORDED, NOT CHANGED: the suggested remedy (parse only the
   leading token, with a stated case policy) changes the field's meaning and is its own round if wanted.
3. My comment said `%h` was only printed, but `records_only(h)` passes it to `git show` -- a lookup of the
   abbreviation git just printed, not a comparison.  COMMENT CORRECTED; the output re-ran byte-identical.
4. The `{7,40}` -> `{6,40}` variant survived Q0-Q6.  Q7 ADDED; it separates the variant.
5. The first driver only printed, so a wrong verdict could not fail it.  VERIFIER ADDED.
   Also noted: Q6 adds no matcher coverage (section L reads the WORKTREE ledger, not the index); Q4's gloss "a shared
   prefix is not a name" was loose -- `cdbe7d76` is NOT a prefix -- corrected in the results file.

 succeeded in 227ms:
   1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
   2: """DOES THE LEDGER KNOW ABOUT THE WORK THAT SHIPPED?
   3: 
   4: Usage:
   5:     check_commit_attribution.py [--list] [--all]
   6: 
   7: The ledger is this project's single source of truth and `gen_dashboard.py` renders the board
   8: from it.  So a row whose work has SHIPPED but whose `commits` field is empty makes the board
   9: under-report finished work and over-report the open queue -- and every decision taken off that
  10: board is then taken off a stale picture.
  11: 
  12: MEASURED 2026-08-19, which is why this exists.  Of the 40 most recent commits, 33 were named
  13: by NO ledger row.  Most of those are legitimate (a receipt, a records fix, harness work that
  14: closes nothing).  The sharp subset is not:
  15: 
  16:     commit 6014b7e   subject "selfmutant6 CLOSED: the fifth control, ..."
  17:                      ledger row `selfmutant6`   state=held   commits=(empty)
  18: 
  19: A commit that announces a row CLOSED, against a row the board still shows as open, is a wrong
  20: record in the direction that matters: it is invisible work.  Thirteen rows were in some form
  21: of this state when the check was written.
  22: 
  23: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  24: WHAT IT CHECKS, and the three grades are deliberately different failures:
  25: 
  26:   HARD   a commit subject names row R with a CLOSURE word, and R.state != "closed".
  27:          EXCEPT when R.reopened names that commit: a closure a later pass of the same round
  28:          superseded, with the board saying so (2026-09-05, pcc2widthkills at b78bb0f).
  29:          The commit says the work is done; the board says it is not.  One of the two is
  30:          wrong and neither can be believed until someone looks.
  31: 
  32:   HARD   R.state == "closed" and a commit subject names R, but R.commits does not list it.
  33:          A closed row that does not name what closed it cannot be attributed -- the same
  34:          defect `nightly_check.sh` fixes for the battery verdict.
  35: 
  36:   SOFT   a commit subject names an OPEN row and R.commits is empty.  Progress commits on an
  37:          open row are ordinary and this is NOT a failure; it is listed so the drift stays
  38:          visible rather than accumulating silently.
  39: 
  40:   SOFT   a commit subject names a CLOSED row whose `commits` is EMPTY.  That is a
  41:          DOCUMENTED-ONLY closure -- a duplicate credited elsewhere, or a row closed with no
  42:          code -- and `check_witness.py` already treats the same shape as legitimate ("nothing
  43:          shipped"), so hard-failing it here would put the two checks in contradiction.  Found
  44:          by this check's FIRST REAL USE, not by a control: rtcdet closed as a duplicate of
  45:          rtcgate with empty commits on purpose, while the commit subjects said "rtcdet", and
  46:          the check demanded the row name a commit it had deliberately declined to claim.
  47: 
  48:          It stays SOFT rather than silent because the shape is also how someone would DODGE
  49:          the check -- close a row that really did ship with empty commits and the hard rule
  50:          evaporates.  Only the closure note distinguishes the two, and only a reader can
  51:          judge it, so the check names the row and declines to grade it.
  52: 
  53: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  54: *** THE BLINDNESS IS REPORTED, NOT HIDDEN, AND THAT IS THE POINT OF THE LAST SECTION. ***
  55: 
  56: Row ids shorter than MIN_ID characters (`n2`, `d1`, `a4`, `b1w`, ...) are NOT matched, because
  57: a two-character token matches English text and would fire on unrelated subjects.  A check that
  58: silently skips a third of its subjects while printing a green line is this project's own named
  59: vacuity class -- "a check whose blindness is reported as a green line".  So the skipped ids are
  60: PRINTED EVERY RUN, with their count, whether or not anything failed.  If you want them covered,
  61: the fix is to give them longer ids, not to lower MIN_ID.
  62: 
  63: *** THE SECOND BLINDNESS: THIS CHECK IS SUBJECT-DRIVEN, so a row is covered only if some
  64: commit happens to NAME it. ***  Found by a negative control that injected three breaks into
  65: `rtcgate` and saw none of them: two commits shipped that row's work and neither subject says
  66: "rtcgate".  Every injection was invisible, and read at first like three detector failures.
  67: A row can therefore have wrong attribution forever and this check will never look at it.
  68: That set is PRINTED EVERY RUN alongside the short-id set, for the same reason: a check that
  69: silently covers a third of its subjects while printing a green line is the vacuity class this
  70: project named.  Widening it (matching commit BODIES, or a `rows:` trailer) is a real option
  71: and is deliberately not taken here -- bodies quote other rows constantly, which would trade
  72: this silent gap for loud false positives.
  73: 
  74: Matching is on the SUBJECT LINE only, at word boundaries.  The body is excluded deliberately:
  75: commit bodies here quote other rows constantly ("filed separately as n2/n4/tfreq"), and a
  76: mention is not a claim of authorship.
  77: 
  78: *** RECORDS-ONLY COMMITS ARE NOT AUTHORSHIP EITHER, AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS CHECK GOT
  79: THAT WRONG. ***  It reported three commits -- ef01e28, 716934d, 8631dc0, all named "m8820x:
  80: ..." -- as a closed row failing to name what closed it.  Verifying instead of believing showed
  81: all three touch ONLY OUTSTANDING_BUGS.md: they are the round's reproduction and research
  82: records, and the row correctly names its five CODE commits.  A row is authored by what shipped,
  83: not by what was written about it.  So a commit whose every path ends in .md is never a hard
  84: failure; it is listed as soft when the row is open, and ignored when the row is closed.
  85: 
  86: That distinction is the check's own version of the rule it exists to serve, and getting it
  87: wrong would have made the gate fire on every research commit this project makes -- the fastest
  88: possible way to have a gate switched off.
  89: """
  90: import io
  91: import json
  92: import os
  93: import re
  94: import subprocess
  95: import sys
  96: 
  97: HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
  98: SEC = os.path.normpath(os.path.join(HERE, "..", ".."))
  99: LEDGER = os.environ.get("GXLEDGER") or os.path.join(HERE, "ledger.json")
 100: 
 101: #  How far back to read.  Not "all history": the ledger's `commits` convention is recent, and
 102: #  older rows were closed under different bookkeeping.  Overridable for an audit.
 103: DEPTH = int(os.environ.get("GXATTR_DEPTH", "60"))
 104: 
 105: #  Below this length a row id is a word, not an identifier.  See the blindness note above.
 106: MIN_ID = 4
 107: 
 108: #  *** A ROW ID THAT IS ORDINARY PROJECT VOCABULARY FALSE-POSITIVES ON PROSE. ***
 109: #  Measured 2026-08-26: commit 2e1e662's subject reads "...and two more carrier
 110: #  claims measured false", where "carrier" means the auto-loaded instruction file.
 111: #  It collided with the CLOSED row `carrier`, the commit carried real code, and this
 112: #  check hard-failed a commit that has nothing to do with that row.
 113: #
 114: #  THE HEADER ABOVE PREDICTS THIS FAILURE FOR THE OTHER FIELD: it declines to match
 115: #  commit BODIES because "bodies quote other rows constantly, which would trade this
 116: #  silent gap for loud false positives."  The unstated assumption was that SUBJECTS
 117: #  do not quote row names as prose.  In a project whose subjects are written in its
 118: #  own vocabulary, they do.
 119: #
 120: #  MIN_ID does not help -- it guards SHORT ids against general English, and this one
 121: #  is seven characters.  The problem is not length but that the token is a common
 122: #  word HERE.
 123: #
 124: #  So for these ids ONLY, a match requires the token to look like an IDENTIFIER --
 125: #  backticked or quoted -- never bare prose.  NOT a blanket exemption: a subject that
 126: #  names `carrier` in backticks still matches and is still graded.  The set is printed
 127: #  every run, because a check that silently skips subjects while printing a green line
 128: #  is the vacuity class this file's own header names.
 129: SUBJECT_STOPWORDS = {"carrier", "census"}   #  "census" added 2026-09-07: same measured
 130:                                             #  class as "carrier" -- a row id that is also
 131:                                             #  an ordinary English word, so a subject using
 132:                                             #  it as PROSE ("my sibling census had a
 133:                                             #  backwards filter") hard-failed against the
 134:                                             #  CLOSED row `census'.  Controlled both ways
 135:                                             #  on the day: the prose subject stops matching,
 136:                                             #  and a backticked `census' STILL matches.
 137: 
 138: #  A commit subject claiming the work is finished.  Deliberately narrow -- "gate", "record"
 139: #  and "file" are progress verbs here, not closure verbs, and including them would turn every
 140: #  ordinary round into a hard failure.
 141: CLOSURE = re.compile(r"\b(closed|closes|closing)\b", re.I)
 142: 
 143: #  *** A LEDGER SHA NAMES A COMMIT WHEN IT IS A PREFIX OF THE FULL HASH -- AND THE FIRST FORM
 144: #  COMPARED ABBREVIATIONS, WHICH GIT LENGTHENS AS THE REPOSITORY GROWS. ***  Measured
 145: #  2026-09-24: the rows carry the 7-character form git printed when each closed; after 4264ee20
 146: #  git's own %h became 8 characters, `h in commits` stopped matching, and six correctly
 147: #  attributed rows hard-failed at HEAD -- a red gate on every commit, raised by nothing in the
 148: #  ledger.  The mirror case failed too: a row written at 8 characters did not match a
 149: #  7-character %h.  Section F's receipt check met this on its first version and matches by
 150: #  prefix; so does this, on %H.  (regress/artifacts/hygienecensus_20260923/attrabbrev_20260924/)
 151: SHA = re.compile(r"(?<![0-9A-Za-z])[0-9a-f]{7,40}(?![0-9A-Za-z])")
 152: 
 153: 
 154: def names(field, full):
 155:     """True when some 7-40 character hex token in `field` is a prefix of the full hash."""
 156:     return any(full.startswith(t) for t in SHA.findall(field or ""))
 157: 
 158: 
 159: def git(*args):
 160:     return subprocess.run(["git", "-C", SEC] + list(args),
 161:                           capture_output=True, text=True).stdout
 162: 
 163: 
 164: def records_only(h):
 165:     """True when every path this commit touched is documentation.
 166: 
 167:     Cheap and cached by the caller: only consulted for commits that already matched a row id,
 168:     which is a handful per run rather than the whole log."""
 169:     paths = [p for p in git("show", "--name-only", "--format=", h).splitlines() if p.strip()]
 170:     #  *** THE LEDGER IS A RECORD, AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS DID NOT KNOW THAT. ***  It
 171:     #  recognised only .md/.txt, so a commit touching ONLY tools/pipeline/ledger.json and
 172:     #  fable_queue.md -- a pure bookkeeping commit that closes a row and queues its held
 173:     #  stage -- was scored as AUTHORSHIP and hard-failed.  Caught on this check's own first
 174:     #  week, by its own output, on a commit written to satisfy a sibling gate.
 175:     #
 176:     #  tools/pipeline/ is the ledger, the queue, the dashboard renderings and the checkers'
 177:     #  own manifests: writing there is recording, never shipping.  Anything OUTSIDE it that
 178:     #  is not .md/.txt is still authorship, so a fix that edits a checker's LOGIC still counts.
 179:     #
 180:     #  *** AND regress/artifacts/ IS A RECORD TOO -- WIDENED 2026-08-31, THE SECOND TIME THIS
 181:     #  PREDICATE HAS BEEN TOO NARROW FOR THE SAME REASON. ***  It fired HARD on 3d30a20, a
 182:     #  records commit whose only non-exempt path was
 183:     #  regress/artifacts/sgideprimop/killtable_20260831/PRETABLE.out -- a pre-table block,
 184:     #  which is evidence written ABOUT a round before its table runs.
 185:     #
 186:     #  That directory holds preserved measurement logs, frozen copies of the tooling a table
 187:     #  ran, session transcripts and pre-table blocks.  `check_probe_wiring.py` already
 188:     #  classifies its python as CLASS_RESEARCH -- in its own words, "evidence, not detectors
 189:     #  the battery owes a run" -- so the two checkers now agree about what that tree is.
 190:     #
 191:     #  The boundary is unchanged and is what keeps this from becoming a hole: a SHIPPED
 192:     #  detector lives in regress/ ITSELF (regress/sgi_re_unmapped_probe.py), not under
 193:     #  artifacts/, and is still authorship.  Only the evidence directory is exempt.
 194:     return bool(paths) and all(
 195:         p.lower().endswith((".md", ".txt"))
 196:         or p.startswith("tools/pipeline/")
 197:         or p.startswith("regress/artifacts/")
 198:         for p in paths)
 199: 
 200: 
 201: #  A `commits` value that is neither a hash list nor a DELIBERATE non-repo note.  Two rows
 202: #  legitimately say "outside the repo (ops)" for work that has no commit at all, so demanding
 203: #  hashes would be wrong -- what must be caught is the PLACEHOLDER: a value written to be
 204: #  replaced later, which nobody replaced.
 205: #
 206: #  MEASURED 2026-08-20: two CLOSED rows carried the literal string "PENDING" and section K
 207: #  accepted them, because check_witness.py skips only an EMPTY commits field.  A field nothing
 208: #  validates is a field that drifts, and this one drifted the same day it was written.
 209: PLACEHOLDER = re.compile(r"^(pending|tbd|todo|xxx|\?+|-+)$", re.I)
 210: NONREPO = re.compile(r"outside the repo|\bops\b|no commit", re.I)
 211: 
 212: 
 213: def bad_commits(rows):
 214:     """Rows whose `commits` is a placeholder rather than hashes or a stated non-repo note."""
 215:     out = []
 216:     for rid, row in sorted(rows.items()):
 217:         c = (row.get("commits") or "").strip()
 218:         if not c or NONREPO.search(c):
 219:             continue
 220:         for tok in c.split():
 221:             if PLACEHOLDER.match(tok) or not re.fullmatch(r"[0-9a-f]{7,40}", tok):
 222:                 out.append((rid, row.get("state", "?"), c))
 223:                 break
 224:     return out
 225: 
 226: 
 227: def main(argv):
 228:     listing = "--list" in argv
 229:     ledger = json.load(io.open(LEDGER, encoding="utf-8"))
 230:     rows = {r["id"]: r for r in ledger["rows"]}
 231: 
 232:     short = sorted(i for i in rows if len(i) < MIN_ID)
 233:     matchable = {i: r for i, r in rows.items() if len(i) >= MIN_ID}
 234:     #  A stopword id must appear as an IDENTIFIER (backticked or quoted); every other
 235:     #  id keeps the word-boundary form.  See SUBJECT_STOPWORDS above for the measured
 236:     #  false positive that motivated this.
 237:     pats = {i: re.compile(
 238:                 (r"[`\"']%s[`\"']" % re.escape(i)) if i in SUBJECT_STOPWORDS
 239:                 else (r"(?<![A-Za-z0-9])%s(?![A-Za-z0-9])" % re.escape(i)))
 240:             for i in matchable}
 241: 
 242:     #  %H to MATCH (see names() above); %h to print, and for records_only()'s own `git show`, where
 243:     #  git resolves the abbreviation it has just printed -- a lookup, never a comparison.
 244:     log = [l.split("\t", 2) for l in
 245:            git("log", "--format=%H\t%h\t%s", "-%d" % DEPTH).splitlines() if l.count("\t") >= 2]
 246: 
 247:     hard, soft, ok = [], [], 0
 248:     for full, h, subj in log:
 249:         for rid, pat in pats.items():
 250:             if not pat.search(subj):
 251:                 continue
 252:             row = matchable[rid]
 253:             commits = (row.get("commits") or "").split()
 254:             state = row.get("state", "")
 255:             #  *** THE CLOSURE CONTRADICTION IS TESTED FIRST, AND THE ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING.
 256:             #  ***  It sat below the `h in commits` early-out until a negative control caught
 257:             #  it: a row can NAME the commit and still contradict it -- state "in work" under
 258:             #  a commit whose subject says CLOSED -- and the early-out scored that as
 259:             #  attributed.  The injected break went undetected and the run stayed green.
 260:             #  Being listed is not the same as agreeing.
 261:             if CLOSURE.search(subj) and state != "closed":
 262:                 #  *** A THIRD CASE, MEASURED 2026-09-05: BOTH WERE RIGHT AT THEIR TIMES. ***  b78bb0f
 263:                 #  closed `pcc2widthkills`; its own pass 2 reopened it the same day and thirteen
 264:                 #  detector passes followed with the fix unchanged.  The board must SAY SO: a row
 265:                 #  whose `reopened` field names the commit is a known reopening and is reported,
 266:                 #  not graded.  Any other closure/state contradiction stays HARD.
 267:                 if names(row.get("reopened"), full):
 268:                     print("  REOPENED %s %-14s %-8s row names this commit as the closure its later passes superseded" % (h, rid, state))
 269:                     ok += 1
 270:                 else:
 271:                     hard.append((h, rid, state, "commit says CLOSED; row is %r" % state, subj))
 272:             elif names(row.get("commits"), full):
 273:                 ok += 1
 274:             elif records_only(h):
 275:                 #  Writing ABOUT a row is not authoring it.  Silent for a closed row (the
 276:                 #  research records of a finished round are not drift); soft for an open one,
 277:                 #  where it is still useful to see the row is being worked.
 278:                 if state != "closed":
 279:                     soft.append((h, rid, state, "records-only commit (.md/.txt)", subj))
 280:             elif state == "closed" and not commits:
 281:                 #  Documented-only closure: see the header.  Named, never graded.
 282:                 soft.append((h, rid, state,
 283:                              "closed documented-only (empty commits) -- read the closure note",
 284:                              subj))
 285:             elif state == "closed":
 286:                 hard.append((h, rid, state, "closed row does not name this code commit", subj))
 287:             elif not commits:
 288:                 #  *** HARD, NOT SOFT -- AND THIS BRANCH USED TO BE SOFT, WHICH IS HOW THE
 289:                 #  CHECK MISSED ITS OWN MOTIVATING CASE. ***  A ninth-seat review measured
 290:                 #  optrow, smdatefmt and constblind all sitting here at once, every one of
 291:                 #  them genuinely shipped, and this tool reported ATTRIBUTION_PASS over the
 292:                 #  top of them.  That is the newest vacuity class -- blindness reported as a
 293:                 #  green line -- inside the check written against exactly that class.  It is
 294:                 #  also the mechanism that produced a 5-0 mis-vote: five seats read a stale
 295:                 #  sentence in a row whose record had not caught up with its code.
 296:                 #
 297:                 #  It is safe to be hard because `commits` is NOT closure-only: twelve OPEN
 298:                 #  rows carry it.  So an open row with a CODE commit naming it and an EMPTY
 299:                 #  field has no legitimate reading -- it is drift, whatever the state says.
 300:                 #  (Records-only and documented-only commits are already filtered above, so
 301:                 #  this fires on shipped CODE alone.)
 302:                 hard.append((h, rid, state,
 303:                              "work shipped and the row does not name it (commits EMPTY)",
 304:                              subj))
 305:             else:
 306:                 soft.append((h, rid, state,
 307:                              "open row, commits=%s" % " ".join(commits), subj))
 308: 
 309:     for label, items in (("HARD", hard), ("SOFT", soft)):
 310:         for h, rid, state, why, subj in items:
 311:             print("  %-6s %-9s %-14s %-8s %s" % (label, h, rid, state, why))
 312:             if listing:
 313:                 print("         %s" % subj[:88])
 314:     print()
 315:     print("  scanned %d commits deep; %d subject/row matches already attributed" % (len(log), ok))
 316:     #  ALWAYS printed, pass or fail -- the blindness is part of the result, not a footnote.
 317:     print("  NOT MATCHABLE (id shorter than %d chars, would match English): %d -- %s"
 318:           % (MIN_ID, len(short), " ".join(short) if short else "none"))
 319:     print("  Rows with these ids are INVISIBLE to this check. Lengthen the id to cover one.")
 320:     #  ALWAYS printed too -- same doctrine as the line above.
 321:     _sw = sorted(i for i in matchable if i in SUBJECT_STOPWORDS)
 322:     print("  STOPWORD IDS (ordinary project vocabulary; matched only when written as\n        an identifier, e.g. backticked): %d -- %s"
 323:           % (len(_sw), " ".join(_sw) if _sw else "none"))
 324: 
 325:     #  The second blindness -- see the header.  A row that SHIPPED but that no commit subject
 326:     #  ever names is never examined, so its attribution could be wrong indefinitely.  Only
 327:     #  rows with commits are worth listing: an open row nobody has committed against yet is
 328:     #  simply not started.
 329:     subjects = " \n".join(sub for _full, _h, sub in log)
 330:     unnamed = sorted(rid for rid, row in matchable.items()
 331:                      if (row.get("commits") or "").strip() and not pats[rid].search(subjects))
 332:     print("  NEVER NAMED in any scanned subject, yet carry commits: %d -- %s"
 333:           % (len(unnamed), " ".join(unnamed) if unnamed else "none"))
 334:     print("  Those rows shipped work this check cannot see. It reads SUBJECTS, not diffs.")
 335: 
 336:     ph = bad_commits(rows)
 337:     if ph:
 338:         print()
 339:         for rid, st, c in ph:
 340:             print("  HARD   %-9s %-14s %-8s commits is not a hash: %r"
 341:                   % ("--", rid, st, c))
 342:         print("  A `commits` value that is neither hashes nor a stated non-repo note is a")
 343:         print("  PLACEHOLDER nobody came back to.  Section K accepts it -- it skips only an")
 344:         print("  EMPTY field -- so this is the only check that sees it.")
 345: 
 346:     if hard or ph:
 347:         print()
 348:         print("ATTRIBUTION_FAIL  %d hard, %d soft, %d placeholder"
 349:               % (len(hard), len(soft), len(ph)))
 350:         return 1
 351:     print("ATTRIBUTION_PASS  0 hard, %d soft" % len(soft))
 352:     return 0

 356:     sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1:]))

   1: #!/bin/sh
   2: 
   3: rm -f tmp_delete.out
   4: LC_ALL=C grep -R "delete " src|grep -v \\.svn|cut -d \: -f 1|sort|uniq |grep -v "inary file" > tmp_delete.out 2> /dev/null
   5: 
   6: ANYERRORS=0
   7: 
   8: for a in `cat tmp_delete.out`; do
   9: 	# Is $a a known exception?
  10: 	if grep -q $a test/check_delete_calls.exceptions; then
  11: 		:
  12: 	else
  13: 		printf "\nError: $a is not in check_delete_calls.exceptions!"
  14: 		ANYERRORS=1
  15: 	fi
  16: done
  17: 
  18: rm -f tmp_delete.out
  19: 
  20: if [ z$ANYERRORS = z1 ]; then
  21: 	printf "\n\n"
  22: 	false
  23: fi

   6: # were written by upstream, before the fork existed, so they encode UPSTREAM's definition
   7: # of correct rather than ours. That independence is the point.
   8: #
   9: # WHAT IT ADDS THAT WE DID NOT ALREADY HAVE. The rigs do load kernels, so loader coverage
  10: # was not zero -- but it was narrow. Measured:
  11: #
  12: #   our rigs load : a.out (bsd.pmax), ELF32 LSB MIPS (arc), ELF32 MSB PPC (macppc),
  13: #                   gzip (landisk .rd)
  14: #   this adds     : ELF64 (RISC-V LSB and SH5 MSB -- a separate code path from ELF32),
  15: #                   b.out i960, MIPS16, and a NEGATIVE case (FileLoader_NonsenseFile),
  16: #                   which is exactly the malformed-input handling the fork hardened
  17: #
  18: # The fork changed SEVEN files under src/file/. Even with this gate, ECOFF, Mach-O, SREC
  19: # and Android still have no corpus here.
  20: #
  21: # TWO MISTAKES THIS GATE MADE ON ITS FIRST RUN, both fixed and both worth recording:
  22: #
  23: #  1. It used `-e testmips`. That is the SUBTYPE flag; the machine type flag is `-E`.
  24: #     Every invocation therefore produced `Sorry, emulation "" (subtype "testmips") is
  25: #     unknown.` -- and the gate compared that identical error across three builds and
  26: #     reported 22 checks passing. Upstream's own configure comment still shows `-e`,
  27: #     which is where the wrong flag came from; it predates the flag change.
  28: #  2. Its only guard was a 100-byte floor on output, which an error message clears
  29: #     easily. A floor proves VOLUME, not VALIDITY. Every case now asserts a
  30: #     format-specific marker that only the real loader path can print -- "ELF64 LSB",
  31: #     "b.out", "a.out" and so on -- so an error, a usage message or an empty run all fail.
  32: #
  33: # DELIBERATELY NOT INCLUDED: test/floatingpoint/fptest.c. It needs a cross-compiler to
  34: # build a program that runs inside the emulator, and gate 2 already proves MORE about the
  35: # one function fix #287 changed: it checks 20 million inputs and shows the difference from
  36: # upstream is exactly what was intended, with nothing extra changed and nothing missed,
  37: # and it has its own self-test proving it can fail. fptest would add the floating-point
  38: # path end to end inside a guest -- worth having, but the most setup for the least new
  39: # information. See README.md.
  40: set -u
  41: HERE=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)
  42: . "$HERE/lib.sh"
  43: 
  44: SEC=$ROOT/GXEMUL-SEC
  45: T=$SEC/test
  46: PRISTINE=/tmp/gx-pristine/gxemul
  47: PREBATCH=/tmp/gx-prebatch/gxemul
  48: HEAD=$ROOT/build/gxemul
  49: 
  50: gate_begin "upstream-test-suite"
  51: need_file "$T"
  52: need_exec "$HEAD"
  53: 
  54: # --- upstream's static hygiene check ---------------------------------
  55: # Every file calling `delete` must appear in its exceptions list. Costs milliseconds.
  56: if [ -f "$T/check_delete_calls.sh" ]; then
  57:     ( cd "$SEC" && sh test/check_delete_calls.sh ) > "$LOGDIR/check_delete.out" 2>&1
  58:     check "upstream check_delete_calls.sh" "$?" "0"
  59:     [ -s "$LOGDIR/check_delete.out" ] && head -6 "$LOGDIR/check_delete.out" | sed 's/^/       /'
  60: else
  61:     degrade "check_delete_calls.sh absent"
  62: fi
  63: echo
  64: 
  65: # --- upstream's loader corpus, three-way ------------------------------
  66: if [ ! -x "$PRISTINE" ] || [ ! -x "$PREBATCH" ]; then
  67:     degrade "baseline builds absent -- run gate_ab.sh --build for the three-way"
  68:     gate_end; exit $?
  69: fi
  70: 
  71: # Normalise the two things that legitimately differ between builds and between runs: the
  72: # version string baked in by configure, and gxemul's random temp filename.
  73: run_case() {   # machine, testfile, binary, outfile
  74:     timeout 20 stdbuf -o0 "$3" -V -E "$1" "$T/$2" </dev/null 2>&1 \
  75:         | sed -e 's|/tmp/gxemul\.[A-Za-z0-9]*|<TMP>|g' \
  76:               -e 's/^GXemul [^ ]*/GXemul <VER>/' > "$4"
  77: }
  78: 
  79: # THE LIST OF DIFFERENCES WE MEANT TO MAKE.
  80: #
  81: # This fork changed about 290 things on purpose, so "upstream and current differ" is not
  82: # by itself a problem -- just counting differences is useless when hundreds are deliberate.
  83: # What makes the comparison workable is sorting each difference into one of three boxes:
  84: #
  85: #     the same                          -> fine
  86: #     differs, and it is fix #N         -> fine, and #N is named below
  87: #     differs, and nobody knows why     -> REGRESSION
  88: #
  89: # Each entry below cancels out exactly one intended change, so anything still left over is
  90: # unexplained by definition. Adding an entry is a deliberate act: it says "we changed this
  91: # on purpose, under this fix number".
  92: #
  93: #   #260 -- net diagnostics were routed through debugmsg(SUBSYS_NET, ...), which prefixes
  94: #           them with "net: ". Upstream printed them bare.
  95: intended_norm() {
  96:     sed -e 's/^\(  *\)net: /\1/'
  97: }
  98: 
  99: note "upstream loader corpus across pristine / pre-batch / HEAD"
 100: note "'raw' = differences vs upstream; 'unexpl' = what remains after known-intended ones"
 101: printf "  %-26s %-11s %-10s %-11s %s\n" "case" "loader-ran" "raw-diff" "unexplained" "pre=HEAD"
 102: 
 103: # machine : file : marker only the real loader path can print
 104: for spec in "testmips:FileLoader_ELF_MIPS:ELF32 MSB" \
 105:             "testmips:FileLoader_ELF_MIPS16:ELF32 MSB" \
 106:             "testriscv:FileLoader_ELF_RISCV64:ELF64 LSB" \
 107:             "testsh:FileLoader_ELF_SH5:ELF64 MSB" \
 108:             "testm88k:FileLoader_A.OUT_M88K:a.out" \
 109:             "barei960:FileLoader_B.OUT_i960:b.out" \
 110:             "testmips:FileLoader_NonsenseFile:symbol"; do
 111:     m=${spec%%:*}; rest=${spec#*:}; f=${rest%%:*}; marker=${rest#*:}
 112:     if [ ! -f "$T/$f" ]; then degrade "$f absent"; continue; fi
 113: 
 114:     run_case "$m" "$f" "$PRISTINE" "$LOGDIR/up_pri.txt"
 115:     run_case "$m" "$f" "$PREBATCH" "$LOGDIR/up_pre.txt"
 116:     run_case "$m" "$f" "$HEAD"     "$LOGDIR/up_head.txt"
 117: 
 118:     ran=$(grep -ac -- "$marker" "$LOGDIR/up_head.txt")
 119: 
 120:     # raw differences, before adjudication
 121:     raw=$(diff "$LOGDIR/up_pri.txt" "$LOGDIR/up_head.txt" | grep -c '^[<>]')
 122: 
 123:     # after erasing every KNOWN-INTENDED change, nothing may remain
 124:     intended_norm < "$LOGDIR/up_pri.txt"  > "$LOGDIR/up_pri_n.txt"
 125:     intended_norm < "$LOGDIR/up_pre.txt"  > "$LOGDIR/up_pre_n.txt"
 126:     intended_norm < "$LOGDIR/up_head.txt" > "$LOGDIR/up_head_n.txt"
 127:     unexplained=$(diff "$LOGDIR/up_pri_n.txt" "$LOGDIR/up_head_n.txt" | grep -c '^[<>]')
 128:     b=$(cmp -s "$LOGDIR/up_pre_n.txt" "$LOGDIR/up_head_n.txt" && echo same || echo DIFF)
 129: 
 130:     printf "  %-26s %-11s %-10s %-11s %s\n" "$f" \
 131:         "$([ "$ran" != 0 ] && echo yes || echo NO)" "$raw" "$unexplained" "$b"
 132: 
 133:     # POSITIVE ASSERTION FIRST. Without it, three builds agreeing on an error message
 134:     # counts as agreement -- which is exactly what this gate did on its first run.
 135:     check_min "$f: loader ran ('$marker')"          "$ran"         1
 136:     check     "$f: unexplained diffs vs upstream"   "$unexplained" "0"
 137:     check     "$f: pre-batch == HEAD"               "$b"           "same"
 138:     if [ "$unexplained" != 0 ] || [ "$b" = DIFF ]; then
 139:         diff "$LOGDIR/up_pri_n.txt" "$LOGDIR/up_head_n.txt" | head -8 | sed 's/^/       /'
 140:     fi
 141: done
 142: 
 143: gate_end
 144: exit $?

#!/bin/sh

rm -f tmp_delete.out
LC_ALL=C grep -R "delete " src|grep -v \\.svn|cut -d \: -f 1|sort|uniq |grep -v "inary file" > tmp_delete.out 2> /dev/null

ANYERRORS=0

for a in `cat tmp_delete.out`; do
	# Is $a a known exception?
	if grep -q $a test/check_delete_calls.exceptions; then
		:
	else
		printf "\nError: $a is not in check_delete_calls.exceptions!"
		ANYERRORS=1
	fi
done

rm -f tmp_delete.out

if [ z$ANYERRORS = z1 ]; then
	printf "\n\n"
	false
fi

 332:     print("  NEVER NAMED in any scanned subject, yet carry commits: %d -- %s"
 333:           % (len(unnamed), " ".join(unnamed) if unnamed else "none"))
 334:     print("  Those rows shipped work this check cannot see. It reads SUBJECTS, not diffs.")
 335: 
 336:     ph = bad_commits(rows)
 337:     if ph:
 338:         print()
 339:         for rid, st, c in ph:
 340:             print("  HARD   %-9s %-14s %-8s commits is not a hash: %r"
 341:                   % ("--", rid, st, c))
 342:         print("  A `commits` value that is neither hashes nor a stated non-repo note is a")
 343:         print("  PLACEHOLDER nobody came back to.  Section K accepts it -- it skips only an")
 344:         print("  EMPTY field -- so this is the only check that sees it.")
 345: 
 346:     if hard or ph:
 347:         print()
 348:         print("ATTRIBUTION_FAIL  %d hard, %d soft, %d placeholder"
 349:               % (len(hard), len(soft), len(ph)))
 350:         return 1
 351:     print("ATTRIBUTION_PASS  0 hard, %d soft" % len(soft))
 352:     return 0
 353: 
 354: 
 355: if __name__ == "__main__":
 356:     sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1:]))

 425:  *  SGI "re", NetBSD sources describes it as a "rendering engine".

 428: DEVICE_ACCESS(sgi_re)
 429: {
 430: 	struct sgi_re_data *d = (struct sgi_re_data *) extra;
 431: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
 432: 
 433: 	idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
 434: 
 435: 	relative_addr += 0x1000;
 436: 
 437: 	/*
 438: 	 *  #497: THE SIX WIDTH GUARDS BELOW USED TO END THE HOST PROCESS.
 439: 	 *
 440: 	 *  A guest choosing an access width is not an emulator error, so each
 441: 	 *  `len != 8' arm now DECLINES the access and announces itself once.
 442: 	 *  Three properties of the shape are load-bearing:
 443: 	 *
 444: 	 *  1. It is an `else if' SPLICE, not an early return.  A `return' here
 445: 	 *     would skip this function's common tail, and `return 0' would be
 446: 	 *     coerced to -1 by cpus/memory_rw.c and raise EXCEPTION_DBE in the
 447: 	 *     guest -- turning a declined access into a guest bus error.
 448: 	 *
 449: 	 *  2. Control therefore reaches the tail, which answers a declined READ
 450: 	 *     with odata (0).
 451: 	 *
 452: 	 *     *** THIS PARAGRAPH ONCE CALLED THAT TAIL DEAD CODE -- "every read arm
 453: 	 *     below still exit(1)s, so odata is never assigned today".  #499 MADE IT
 454: 	 *     LIVE: the unmatched-address catch-all no longer exits, so a declined
 455: 	 *     read now runs the tail and lands a defined 0, and the shipped detector's
 456: 	 *     `rd sent' row exists to pin exactly that.  The six `TODO: read from'
 457: 	 *     arms below DO still exit(1) -- that half is unchanged and is sgirekills. ***
 458: 	 *
 459: 	 *     *** AND THAT HALF IS NOW GONE TOO: #513 REMOVED ALL SIX.  The tail is
 460: 	 *     reached by more than declines now -- five of the six arms ASSIGN odata
 461: 	 *     and let the tail deliver it, so the tail is the ACCEPT path as well as
 462: 	 *     the decline path.  The sentence above is #499's and was true when
 463: 	 *     written; it stands with its correction, as #499's own does. ***
 464: 	 *
 465: 	 *  3. The guards are NOT redundant.  For TLB_A/B/C and TEX the store is a
 466: 	 *     `for (hwi = 0; hwi < len; ...)' loop, so a narrower access simply
 467: 	 *     runs fewer iterations and stays in bounds -- there the guard is
 468: 	 *     modelling fidelity only.  For LINEAR_A and LINEAR_B it is not; see
 469: 	 *     the note at those two sites.
 470: 	 *
 471: 	 *  Only the WIDTH kills change HERE, IN #497.  The six `TODO: read from ...'
 472: 	 *  kills are still untouched.
 473: 	 *
 474: 	 *  *** UNTRUE SINCE #513, WHICH IS THE ROUND THAT TOOK THEM.  Five became
 475: 	 *  ACCEPTs (the read returns what the write stored) and CRIME_RE_TEX became a
 476: 	 *  latched decline.  #497's sentence was accurate for #497 and is kept for the
 477: 	 *  same reason the catch-all correction below is kept: a reader arriving from
 478: 	 *  #497's records has to be able to see which half moved and when. ***
 479: 	 *
 480: 	 *  *** THE CATCH-ALL IS NO LONGER UNTOUCHED: #499 DE-FATALISED IT and that row
 481: 	 *  is CLOSED.  The sentence is #497's and was true when written; it is left
 482: 	 *  standing with its correction rather than rewritten, because a reader
 483: 	 *  arriving from #497's records needs to see which half moved. ***  The READ
 484: 	 *  arms are
 485: 	 *  `sgirekills`, the CATCH-ALL is `sgireunmapped` (two different rows: one is a
 486: 	 *  direction, the other an unmatched address with no width predicate at all), as is
 487: 	 *  DEVICE_ACCESS(sgi_de)'s own width kill, whose accept set is {4,8}
 488: 	 *  rather than {8} (sgidewidth).
 489: 	 */
 490: 	if (relative_addr >= CRIME_RE_TLB_A && relative_addr < CRIME_RE_TLB_B) {
 491: 		if (len != 8) {
 492: 			if (!(d->warned & SGIRE_W_TLBA_LEN)) {
 493: 				d->warned |= SGIRE_W_TLBA_LEN;
 494: 				fatal("[ sgi_re: unimplemented len=%i for "
 495: 				    "CRIME_RE_TLB_A -- declined.  (once) ]\n",
 496: 				    (int) len);
 497: 			}
 498: 		} else if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 499: 			int tlbi = ((relative_addr & 0x1ff) >> 1) & 0xff;
 500: 			for (size_t hwi = 0; hwi < len; hwi += sizeof(uint16_t)) {
 501: 				d->re_tlb_a[tlbi] = data[hwi]*256 + data[hwi+1];
 502: 				debug("d->re_tlb_a[%i] = 0x%04x\n", tlbi, d->re_tlb_a[tlbi]);
 503: 				tlbi++;
 504: 			}
 505: 		} else {
 506: 			/*
 507: 			 *  #513: ACCEPT.  A guest read of a len == 8 TLB slot ended
 508: 			 *  the host.  The write arm directly above already computes
 509: 			 *  and stores the value, so the disposition is member 4 of
 510: 			 *  the option set -- remove the kill and RETURN THE VALUE --
 511: 			 *  rather than a decline: nothing is unimplemented here.
 512: 			 *  This loop is the exact inverse of the write loop, cell for
 513: 			 *  cell and shift for shift.
 514: 			 *
 515: 			 *  *** IT MUST WRITE odata, NOT data[]. ***  The function's
 516: 			 *  common tail is
 517: 			 *      if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 518: 			 *              memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
 519: 			 *  so anything stored into data[] here is overwritten before
 520: 			 *  it reaches the guest.  A first draft of this fix filled
 521: 			 *  data[] and would have returned zeroes while looking right.
 522: 			 */
 523: 			int tlbi = ((relative_addr & 0x1ff) >> 1) & 0xff;
 524: 			for (size_t hwi = 0; hwi < len; hwi += sizeof(uint16_t)) {
 525: 				odata = (odata << 16) | d->re_tlb_a[tlbi];
 526: 				tlbi++;
 527: 			}
 528: 		}
 529: 	} else if (relative_addr >= CRIME_RE_TLB_B && relative_addr < CRIME_RE_TLB_C) {
 530: 		if (len != 8) {
 531: 			if (!(d->warned & SGIRE_W_TLBB_LEN)) {
 532: 				d->warned |= SGIRE_W_TLBB_LEN;
 533: 				fatal("[ sgi_re: unimplemented len=%i for "
 534: 				    "CRIME_RE_TLB_B -- declined.  (once) ]\n",
 535: 				    (int) len);
 536: 			}
 537: 		} else if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 538: 			int tlbi = ((relative_addr & 0x1ff) >> 1) & 0xff;
 539: 			for (size_t hwi = 0; hwi < len; hwi += sizeof(uint16_t)) {
 540: 				d->re_tlb_b[tlbi] = data[hwi]*256 + data[hwi+1];
 541: 				debug("d->re_tlb_b[%i] = 0x%04x\n", tlbi, d->re_tlb_b[tlbi]);
 542: 				tlbi++;
 543: 			}
 544: 		} else {
 545: 			/*  #513: ACCEPT -- the inverse of the write loop.  See the
 546: 			    CRIME_RE_TLB_A arm for why this sets odata, not data[].  */
 547: 			int tlbi = ((relative_addr & 0x1ff) >> 1) & 0xff;
 548: 			for (size_t hwi = 0; hwi < len; hwi += sizeof(uint16_t)) {
 549: 				odata = (odata << 16) | d->re_tlb_b[tlbi];
 550: 				tlbi++;
 551: 			}
 552: 		}
 553: 	} else if (relative_addr >= CRIME_RE_TLB_C && relative_addr < CRIME_RE_TLB_C + 0x200) {

 555: 			if (!(d->warned & SGIRE_W_TLBC_LEN)) {
 556: 				d->warned |= SGIRE_W_TLBC_LEN;

VERDICT: U=SHIP WITH NOTES; R=SHIP WITH NOTES

1. **U-1 — The edit fixes the identified subcheck; two matching premises need correction.** The search pattern is exactly `(d, e, l, e, t, e, space)`, without language parsing or word boundaries. It subsequently excludes lines containing `.svn`. Binary-match messages on stderr bypass the `"inary file"` filter—the supplied battery output demonstrates that.

   I enumerated HEAD’s matching text files under `src/`:

   | File | Matching lines | Exception result |
   |---|---|---|
   | `debugger/debugger.c` | 688 | Exempt |
   | `debugger/debugger_cmds.c` | 66, 119, 335, 389 | Exempt |
   | `include/refcount_ptr.h` | 101, 274 | Exempt |
   | `devices/dev_sgi_re.c` | 510 | Not exempt |

   Removing U’s occurrence leaves no unmatched file in this enumeration. However, `grep -q $a` is **an unanchored basic regular expression**, not literal substring matching. Its dots are wildcards. The `.cc` exceptions consequently cover the fork’s `.c` debugger paths.

2. **U-2 — Rewording is the best option.** Adding an exception would exempt every future matching line in this entire source file, including actual code. Changing the checker introduces broader behavior changes. Another option is deleting the redundant sentence, which offers no advantage over this word replacement. Existing fork comments already use “drop the exit(1),” including [dev_sh4.c:1409](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sh4.c:1409).

3. **U-3 — File the precommit landing spot; it need not enlarge U.** This static check is cheap and useful. A sound integration must check the staged candidate and avoid concurrent use of its fixed `tmp_delete.out`.
